---

## High-Level Elegance Review (Refresh)

**Score: 84% -> 88%** (+4 points)

### What improved since last review

1. **core/constants.py indirection eliminated**: The previous review flagged `core/constants.py` as a reverse dependency (core importing from engine). The indirection has been removed -- all 13 consumers of `ATTEST_EXAMPLE` now import directly from `engine.work_queue_internal.helpers` or `.core`. The file itself remains but is completely dead (zero importers). This resolves the prior "core_imports_engine" finding.

2. **Dead duplicate sections.py removed**: Reduces confusion about canonical module locations.

3. **Deprecation warnings added to deprecated CLI flags**: Proper lifecycle management.

4. **Tree-sitter compatibility shim removed**: Dead code cleanup.

### Architecture strengths confirmed

The architecture is genuinely strong:

- **Domain-aligned packages**: `core/` (shared runtime/config/registry), `engine/` (detectors/scoring/state internals), `intelligence/` (narrative/review), `app/` (commands/output/CLI), `languages/` (plugins) -- each with clear ownership.
- **Facade pattern**: `state.py` (71 lines), `scoring.py` (67 lines), `engine/planning/core.py` (15 lines) re-export stable public APIs from internal subpackages with explicit `__all__`.
- **Import linter enforcement**: 3 contracts in `.importlinter` prevent cross-layer violations (engine.detectors cannot import languages or intelligence.review; narrative cannot import app.commands).
- **Plugin auto-discovery**: `languages/framework/discovery.py` dynamically loads `plugin_*.py` files, language packages, and user plugins from `.desloppify/plugins/`.
- **Canonical detector registry**: `core/registry.py` is the single source of truth for all detector metadata with frozen dataclasses, runtime registration callbacks, and derived views.
- **Clean command dispatch**: `app/commands/registry.py` maps command names to handlers in a simple dict.
- **Well-decomposed scan pipeline**: `scan.py` (160 LOC) delegates to `scan_workflow.py`, `scan_helpers.py`, and 6 `scan_reporting_*` modules.
- **Runtime context isolation**: `core/runtime_state.py` uses `contextvars` for proper test isolation and concurrent safety.

### Remaining findings

1. **`core/constants.py` is dead code** (high confidence): The file still exists but has zero importers. It should be deleted entirely.

2. **engine->intelligence cross-layer import** (medium confidence, wontfixed from prior review): `engine/state_internal/scoring.py` imports `matches_target_score` from `intelligence.integrity.subjective`. This is the sole upward dependency from engine to intelligence. The function is pure (a float comparison) and could be moved to `engine.scoring_internal.policy` to eliminate the violation.

3. **Scan reporting module proliferation** (low confidence): 6 `scan_reporting_*` modules is on the high end of decomposition for what is essentially "print scan results." However, the naming is consistent, each module has a clear domain slice, and the orchestrator (`scan.py`) is clean. This is defensible as-is.

### Output

Review JSON written to `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.desloppify/review_high_level_elegance.json` with score **88** and 3 findings.</result>

<summary>Agent "Re-review error_consistency dim" completed</summary>
<result>## Error Consistency Review -- Score: 88/100

### Assessment Summary

The desloppify codebase demonstrates genuinely strong error handling engineering. After reading 20+ files across all layers (CLI, state persistence, scoring, language plugins, external tool adapters, fixers, review workflows), here is the evidence-based assessment.

### Strengths (well-established patterns)

1. **Dedicated fallback infrastructure** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/fallbacks.py`): Four centralized helpers -- `log_best_effort_failure`, `warn_best_effort`, `print_error`, `restore_files_best_effort` -- provide a consistent vocabulary for non-fatal failures.

2. **No bare except in production code**: Grep confirms bare `except:` appears only in test fixture strings. All production handlers catch specific exception types.

3. **Atomic writes with cleanup** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/utils.py` lines 92-104): `safe_write_text()` uses temp file + `os.replace` with cleanup on failure. Used consistently by state persistence, config saves, fixer output, and visualization writes.

4. **State corruption recovery** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/persistence.py`): `load_state` handles corrupted JSON with backup fallback, corrupted backup, version mismatch -- a well-layered recovery chain catching `json.JSONDecodeError`, `UnicodeDecodeError`, `OSError`, and `ValueError`.

5. **Consistent subprocess handling**: All `subprocess.run` calls include `timeout`, `capture_output=True`, `text=True`. External tool adapters (Knip, ruff, jscpd) follow a uniform pattern: catch `FileNotFoundError` (tool not installed) and `TimeoutExpired`, return `None` to signal graceful degradation.

6. **Validation with context preservation**: `validate_state_invariants` in schema.py raises `ValueError` with the specific finding ID. `parse_holistic_import_payload` raises `ValueError` with structured messages. Error context is consistently preserved.

7. **Graceful degradation for optional tools**: Scorecard (PIL), Knip (npx), ruff, jscpd, tree-sitter -- all optional dependencies degrade gracefully to `None` without crashing.

8. **Consistent sys.exit patterns**: CLI commands use `sys.exit(1)` for user errors and `sys.exit(2)` for usage errors, a standard convention applied uniformly across resolve, config, review, and fix commands.

### Findings (minor issues)

1. **`broad_exception_in_execute_batches`** (medium confidence): In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py` line 287, `except (RuntimeError, OSError, ValueError, Exception)` includes bare `Exception` which makes the specific types redundant. This is the only catch-all in production code.

2. **`inconsistent_warning_format_state_save`** (low confidence): `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/persistence.py` line 147 uses ad-hoc `print(f"  Warning: ...")` instead of the centralized `fallbacks.warn_best_effort()` or `fallbacks.print_error()`. Similarly in `visualize.py` line 200.

3. **`subprocess_missing_oserror_catch_ruff_jscpd`** (medium confidence): The knip_adapter catches `FileNotFoundError`, `TimeoutExpired`, AND `OSError` (three layers), while `ruff_smells.py` and `jscpd_adapter.py` only catch the first two. This means permission-denied or broken-pipe errors would propagate unhandled in those two adapters.

4. **`emoji_in_corruption_messages`** (low confidence): State corruption messages in persistence.py use Unicode warning emoji directly, while `fallbacks.py` uses text prefixes like `WARNING:` and `Error:`. Cosmetic inconsistency.

### Why 88% (up from 87%)

The previous wontfixed items remain appropriately wontfixed (PROJECT_ROOT graceful degradation, treesitter silent swallow, scorecard silent failure, etc.). The codebase error handling is genuinely well-engineered with:
- A clear centralized fallback infrastructure that most code uses
- No bare except, no Exception-only catches (except the one ThreadPoolExecutor case)
- Consistent subprocess timeout handling
- Atomic writes everywhere
- State corruption recovery with backup chain

The score moves from 87 to 88 because the recent codebase changes (atomic writes in fixers, consistent subprocess timeouts, structured error returns in import_helpers) have slightly improved the already-strong foundation. The remaining findings are minor: one catch-all Exception, a couple ad-hoc warning formats, and a subprocess catch consistency gap in 2 of 4 adapters.</result>

---

## Fix 2: Broad except in `execute_batches`

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py`** (line 287)

Changed:
```python
except (RuntimeError, OSError, ValueError, Exception) as exc:
```
To:
```python
except Exception as exc:  # future.result() can raise any exception from the batch runner
```

Since `Exception` is the base class of `RuntimeError`, `OSError`, and `ValueError`, listing them all is redundant. The comment explains the intent: `future.result()` re-raises whatever exception the batch runner threw, so a broad catch is deliberate here.

---

## Fix 3: Subprocess missing `OSError` catch

`FileNotFoundError` is a subclass of `OSError`, but other `OSError` variants (e.g., `PermissionError`, `ProcessLookupError`, or platform-specific errors) could occur when spawning subprocesses.

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/ruff_smells.py`** (after line 88)

Added:
```python
except OSError as exc:
    logger.debug("ruff smells: OS error running ruff: %s", exc)
    return None
```

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/jscpd_adapter.py`** (after line 132)

Added:
```python
except OSError as exc:
    logger.debug("jscpd: OS error running npx/jscpd: %s", exc)
    return None
```

In both files, the `OSError` handler is placed after `FileNotFoundError` (which is more specific) and before `subprocess.TimeoutExpired`, maintaining proper exception hierarchy ordering.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review low_level_elegance" completed</summary>
<result>Here is a summary of the review:

**Score: 82 / 100**

The codebase has good low-level hygiene overall — functions are generally focused, control flow is readable, and the recent round of improvements (narrative splitting, `_scan_code()` generator, scoring consolidation) cleaned up many earlier issues. The four confirmed findings that keep the score below the 90s are:

---

**Finding 1 — `detect_complexity_try_except_dispatch` (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/complexity.py`, lines 45-48

`sig.compute(content, lines, _filepath=filepath)` is wrapped in a `try/except TypeError` that retries without `_filepath` on failure. This is exception-driven signature probing: any real `TypeError` inside a compute function is silently swallowed and a second (possibly wrong) call is made. The fix is to standardize all `ComplexitySignal.compute` callables to accept `**kwargs` or `_filepath=None`, then call them uniformly.

---

**Finding 2 — `is_entrypoint_file_redundant_checks` (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/csharp/detectors/deps.py`, lines 221-238

`_PLATFORM_BASE_RE` and `_PLATFORM_REGISTER_RE` are each evaluated twice: once inside the `is_platform_path` guard (line 228) and again unconditionally (lines 235-237). For files on a platform path, the two bottom checks duplicate work already done. The control flow implies they are independent conditions, when they are actually a superset of the earlier guarded check.

---

**Finding 3 — `match_is_in_string_incomplete_prefix_handling` (medium confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells.py`, lines 249-289 vs 174-246

`_match_is_in_string` handles only 1-character string prefixes (`r`, `b`, `f`); `_build_string_line_set` handles both 1- and 2-character prefix combinations (`rb`, `fr`, etc.). The asymmetry means `_match_is_in_string` can fail to recognize a match position as inside a `rb'...'` or `fr'...'` literal. A shared `_skip_string_prefix()` helper would fix both correctness and duplication.

---

**Finding 4 — `reset_subjective_setdefault_then_isinstance` (medium confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`, lines 123-126

`state.setdefault('subjective_assessments', {})` is immediately followed by `if not isinstance(assessments, dict): assessments = {}`. `setdefault` implies "ensure a dict is present", yet the very next two lines suggest we distrust what it returned and may overwrite it anyway. The intent is clearer as an explicit get-and-coerce.

---

**Finding 5 — `missing_blank_lines_between_functions_apply_flow` (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`

Every pair of top-level function definitions in this file is separated by zero blank lines (at boundaries on lines 37/38, 61/62, 138/139, 179/180, 194/195, 221/222). PEP 8 requires two blank lines between top-level definitions. This is a consistent formatting issue across the entire file.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review mid_level_elegance" completed</summary>
<result>The review is complete. Here is a summary of what I found and the final assessment:

---

**Score: 90 / 100**

The codebase's module boundaries and integration seams are, overall, well-designed. The layer architecture is clear and consistent: `app/commands` talks to the `engine` via the `desloppify.state` public facade, language plugins are accessed through `desloppify.languages`, scoring is consumed via `desloppify.scoring`, and the narrative layer is pure/functional. Several areas demonstrate excellent handoff design:

- `LangRun`/`LangConfig`/`LangRunOverrides` form a clean immutable config + mutable runtime separation that all phases accept uniformly.
- `ScanRuntime`, `ScanMergeResult`, `ScanNoiseSnapshot` clearly package data across the scan workflow phases without ambient state.
- The `state.py` facade cleanly re-exports from `engine.state_internal.*` — all callers should go through it, and almost all do.
- `NarrativeContext` as a frozen dataclass input to `compute_narrative()` is a clean contract.
- The `FixerConfig` and `DetectorPhase` dataclasses provide clear, typed handoffs through the fixer and detector pipelines.

**Two confirmed issues deducted:**

1. **`app/commands/review/import_helpers.py` imports `_coerce_assessment_score` directly from `engine.state_internal.resolution`** — a private symbol from a deep internal module, bypassing the public `desloppify.state` facade. This is the only place in the `app/commands` layer that reaches into `engine.state_internal.*` directly for a non-schema type. It represents a real layer boundary breach.

2. **`fix/io.py` has a wrong return type annotation on `_load_state`** — declared as `tuple[str, dict]` but actually returning `tuple[Path | None, dict]` since `state_path()` returns `Path | None`. `_save_state` also annotates its path parameter as `str` while passing it to `save_state(path: Path | None)`. The runtime behavior is correct (Path is compatible with save_state), but the false annotation is a contract lie at this particular seam.</result>

## Contract Coherence Review — Summary

**Score: 90/100**

Output written to `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.desloppify/review_contract_coherence.json`.

### Review Scope

I examined approximately 60 files covering:
- The `state.py` / `engine/state_internal/` facade chain
- The `scoring.py` / `engine/scoring_internal/` facade chain
- The `intelligence/narrative/` package (core, reminders, phase, headline, dimensions, strategy, actions)
- `languages/framework/` (types, runtime, resolution, discovery, contract_validation)
- `utils.py`, `core/runtime_state.py`, `core/registry.py`, `core/enums.py`
- App output modules (`visualize.py`, `scorecard.py`)

### What is Clean

- All previously reported issues from the prior review run are confirmed fixed: `NarrativeResult.reminders` type is correctly `list[dict[str, Any]]`; `visualize.py __all__` includes `cmd_viz` and `cmd_tree`; `get_dimension_for_detector` has a `-> Dimension | None` annotation; `display_entries` docstring is accurate.
- `state.py` facade `__all__` exactly matches its imports.
- `scoring.py` facade `__all__` exactly matches its imports.
- `narrative/__init__.py __all__` matches all re-exported names.
- `_compute_reminders` correctly returns `tuple[list[dict], dict]` matching all consumers.
- `load_state`, `save_state`, `merge_scan`, `resolve_findings`, `match_findings` all honor their declared return types and docstrings.
- `LangConfig` and `LangRun` contracts are validated at runtime via `contract_validation.py`.
- `compute_tool_hash`, `safe_write_text`, `rel`, `matches_exclusion` all match declared behavior.

### Confirmed Issues

**1. `narrative_result_milestone_type` (high confidence)**
`NarrativeResult.milestone` is typed `dict[str, Any] | None` (core.py line 308), but `_detect_milestone()` returns `str | None` and all call sites — including `resolve/cmd.py` using it in an f-string — treat it as a string. The TypedDict annotation is wrong.

Fix: `milestone: str | None` in `NarrativeResult`.

**2. `narrative_result_headline_nullability` (high confidence)**
`NarrativeResult.headline` is typed `str` (core.py line 302, non-optional), but `_compute_headline()` returns `str | None` and does explicitly return `None` when there is no content to display. The returned dict at `compute_narrative()` assigns this `None`-capable value directly to the `"headline"` key with no coercion.

Fix: Either `headline: str | None` in the TypedDict, or add `headline = headline or ""` before the return.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review error_consistency" completed</summary>
<result>Here is the full writeup of the review.

---

## error_consistency Review

**Score: 91 / 100**

The error handling strategy across this codebase is disciplined, consistent, and well-layered. The vast majority of exception sites are correct, specific, and use a shared set of utilities (`log_best_effort_failure`, `warn_best_effort`, `print_error` from `core/fallbacks.py`). Two genuine issues were confirmed.

---

### What is working well

**Subprocess calls.** Every external-process adapter (`knip_adapter.py`, `ruff_smells.py`, `jscpd_adapter.py`, `bandit_adapter.py`, `import_linter_adapter.py`, `unused.py`) uses the same three-exception pattern: `FileNotFoundError` → tool not installed (graceful skip), `OSError` → OS-level failure, `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` → timeout. All have `timeout=` parameters. All return `None` on failure to signal "skip this detector" rather than propagating.

**State persistence.** `persistence.py` implements full backup/restore, atomic writes via `safe_write_text`, and separate catches for JSON corruption vs. OS failures. The `load_state` fallback chain (backup → fresh start) is correctly guarded. `save_state` re-raises after printing, preserving the failure mode for callers.

**File I/O in detectors.** Every file-reading loop in detectors (complexity, coverage mapping, graph, security) catches `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` and logs at `debug` level, then continues. No silent drops.

**Error chaining.** All re-raises use `raise ... from exc` (e.g., `dimensions/data.py`, `languages/framework/resolution.py`, `review/context_signals/migration.py`), preserving the original traceback.

**The `Exception` catch in `runner_helpers.py` is intentional and correct.** The comment explains why: `future.result()` on a `ThreadPoolExecutor` future can re-raise any exception from the worker thread. Catching `Exception` here is the standard pattern for futures and is documented in-place.

---

### Finding 1 — Dead exception type (high confidence)

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`, line 219

```python
except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
    return
```

`subprocess.CalledProcessError` cannot be raised by `subprocess.run()` unless `check=True` is passed. The call on line 198 uses no `check=True`:

```python
r = subprocess.run(
    ["git", "status", "--porcelain"], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5
)
```

The `CalledProcessError` entry is dead code. Every other `subprocess.run` call in the codebase correctly omits it; `scorecard_parts/meta.py` correctly includes it because `subprocess.check_output` is used there, which does raise on non-zero exit.

**Fix:** Remove `subprocess.CalledProcessError` from the tuple.

---

### Finding 2 — TypeError used as a signature probe (medium confidence)

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/complexity.py`, lines 45-48

```python
try:
    result = sig.compute(content, lines, _filepath=filepath)
except TypeError:
    result = sig.compute(content, lines)
```

This uses `TypeError` (unexpected keyword argument) as a runtime signal to determine whether the `compute` function accepts the `_filepath` parameter. The pattern works correctly today because all current compute functions either use `sig.pattern` only (no `compute`) or are tree-sitter closures that accept `_filepath` as a keyword-only default arg.

The risk: a `TypeError` raised *inside* `sig.compute(content, lines, _filepath=filepath)` after successfully accepting the arguments would be caught here and silently cause fallback to the 2-arg form — producing wrong or missing results rather than surfacing the error.

**Fix:** Use `inspect.signature(sig.compute).parameters` to detect the `_filepath` parameter at `ComplexitySignal` construction time, or add an `accepts_filepath: bool` attribute to `ComplexitySignal` directly.</result>

---

## Review: `ai_generated_debt` dimension

**Score: 89 / 100**

The codebase is generally free of the worst AI-generation hallmarks. Comments explain "why" rather than restating "what the next line does". Docstrings are short and purposeful. There are no obvious defensive overengineering patterns like nested try/except on operations that cannot fail, no gratuitous type-checking on values that are guaranteed by construction, and no `if x is not None and len(x) > 0` verbosity. The architecture is clean, with a genuine consolidation effort evident throughout (shared phases, `_constants.py`, `DetectorMeta` registry, `FixerConfig`, etc.).

Two confirmed, verified issues were found:

### Finding 1: `score_loading_boilerplate` (confidence: high)

The 4-score loading pattern — calling `get_overall_score`, `get_objective_score`, `get_strict_score`, and `get_verified_strict_score` in sequence — is duplicated across 7 files in `app/commands/fix/`, `app/commands/resolve/`, and `app/commands/scan/`. `scan_reporting_llm.py` already has a private `_load_scores()` that bundles the four calls, but it is module-private and not reused by any of the other sites.

**Suggestion:** Expose a shared `score_snapshot(state)` function in `desloppify/engine/state_internal/schema.py` or `desloppify/state.py`.

### Finding 2: `bool_isinstance_guard_repeated` (confidence: high)

The guard `isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, int | float)` (used to safely coerce a JSON payload to a numeric score, rejecting bools and non-numerics) appears inline in 11 places across 9 files. Two files (`persistence.py` and `resolution.py`) already define private helper functions implementing exactly this logic, but neither is imported by the other 7 sites.

**Suggestion:** Promote `_coerce_assessment_score` from `resolution.py` to a shared helper in `schema.py` and replace all inline guards.

---

**What was ruled out:** The `_tree_safety_detectors.py` re-export aliases (`_detect_X as _detect_X`) look verbose but are a deliberate ruff-compatibility idiom, not boilerplate. The `_coerce_value` method in `LangConfig` has legitimate branching for bool-vs-numeric disambiguation at the coercion level (not the score-guard level), so it was not flagged.</result>

The detector functions (`_detect_constant_return`, `_detect_unreachable_code`, etc.), data classes (`MergeScanOptions`, `ScanRuntime`, `QueueBuildOptions`), and public APIs (`merge_scan`, `compute_narrative`, `build_work_queue`) all name their purpose accurately. Type aliases (`DepGraphBuilder`, `FileFinder`, `FunctionExtractor`) add clarity.

**One confirmed issue — `sp` parameter in review/batch function signatures:**

The name `sp` (for "state file path") persists as an **actual parameter name** in three public function signatures:

<summary>Agent "Review high_level_elegance" completed</summary>
<result>Here is a detailed writeup of the review.

---

## High-Level Elegance Review — Results

**Score: 84/100**

The overall architecture is in very good shape. The layer boundaries (`app → engine → intelligence → languages`) are almost entirely respected. The consolidation into `registry.py`, the narrative package split, and the facade pattern in `state.py` / `scoring.py` are all clean. Three confirmed issues were found:

---

### Finding 1: engine imports intelligence (layer violation)
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/scoring.py`, line 10

```python
from desloppify.intelligence.integrity.subjective import matches_target_score
```

This is the only upward dependency from `engine` into `intelligence` in the entire codebase. The function itself is a pure 3-line float comparison with no domain-specific logic — it belongs in `core` (alongside `enums.py`, `fallbacks.py`) or in `engine/scoring_internal` itself. The fix is to move `matches_target_score` and `SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_MATCH_TOLERANCE` to `desloppify/core/` and update all three call sites.

---

### Finding 2: Duplicate display-name mapping
**Files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/subjective/core.py` (`DISPLAY_NAMES`, lines 9–31)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/metadata.py` (`_LEGACY_DISPLAY_NAMES`, lines 14–34)

Both contain an identical 19-entry dict mapping dimension keys like `"high_level_elegance"` to display strings like `"High Elegance"`. The `metadata.py` copy is described as "backward-compatible defaults" but is never derived from the canonical `DISPLAY_NAMES`. Adding a new dimension or renaming a display string requires updating two places. The fix is to have `metadata.py` import `DISPLAY_NAMES` from `engine/scoring_internal/subjective/core.py` (a valid downward import) as its fallback.

---

### Finding 3: Dead module `engine/detectors/lang_hooks.py`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/lang_hooks.py`

This 23-line file defines `load_lang_hook_module` which is never called from any other file in the codebase. The actively-used hook system is `desloppify/hook_registry.py`, which is imported by `engine/detectors/review_coverage.py`, `engine/detectors/coverage/mapping.py`, `engine/detectors/test_coverage/heuristics.py`, and all four language `__init__.py` files that register hooks. `lang_hooks.py` appears to be a superseded predecessor that was not deleted when `hook_registry.py` was introduced. It should be deleted.</result>

## Abstraction Fitness Review — Summary

**Score: 88/100**

I reviewed all 569 Python files in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/` for unnecessary indirection, thin forwarding modules, and over-abstracted helpers. The file has been written to `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.desloppify/review_abstraction_fitness.json`.

### What is healthy (no deductions)

- The canonical detector registry (`core/registry.py`) is a clean single source of truth
- The DI-binding wrappers in `scan_reporting_dimensions.py` (e.g. `show_detector_progress`, `show_score_model_breakdown`) are intentional — they bind injectable dependencies for testability, not pure indirection
- `_scorecard_dimension_rows` in `scan_reporting_dimensions.py` is a monkeypatch seam for tests, not pointless indirection
- The `narrative/` package split is clean with proper re-exports via `__init__.py`
- `utils.py` facade over `core.internal.text_utils` is an accepted public API surface
- The `helpers/` command modules (`lang.py`, `runtime.py`, `state.py`, etc.) are genuine domain helpers, not thin forwarders

### Confirmed findings

**High confidence:**

1. **`resolve_detection_root_private_stub`** — `_resolve_detection_root` in `helpers/lang.py` (lines 99-101) is a one-line private stub wrapping the public `resolve_detection_root` with no added logic. Its only call site is `auto_detect_lang_name()` one line below. Should be deleted.

2. **`make_get_detect_commands_factory_of_factory`** — `make_get_detect_commands` in `framework/commands_base.py` (lines 295-316) returns a closure that wraps `build_standard_detect_registry` with the same arguments. All three callers (csharp, dart, gdscript) invoke the returned closure immediately (`detect_commands=get_detect_commands()`), so no deferred evaluation benefit exists. The extra factory layer adds zero value.

3. **`registry_order_separate_module`** — `core/registry_order.py` is a 31-line file containing only `DISPLAY_ORDER = [...]`, imported solely by `core/registry.py`. No circular dependency prevents inlining.

**Medium confidence:**

4. **`phase_builders_trivial_factories`** — `phase_builders.py` has 6 one-liner factory functions that simply construct `DetectorPhase(label, fn)`. They protect against label drift but are thin enough to consider inlining.

5. **`scorecard_subjective_entries_split_impl`** — Two separate implementations of `scorecard_subjective_entries` exist in `projection.py` (canonical, richer schema) and `scan_reporting_dimensions.py` (filtered thinned copy). The `scan_reporting_dimensions` version is used as a DI callback by `show/render.py` and `review/output.py` but adds no distinct logic over the `projection.py` canonical version.</result>

<result>Here is a summary of the review:

---

**Score: 68/100**

Five verified findings were identified. Two are new discoveries; three carry over from the previous review with confirmed current validity.

---

**Finding 1: `dead_phase_quality_module` (high confidence)**

`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/_phase_quality.py` is a dead stranded module with three stale duplicate implementations of phase functions (`_phase_mutable_state`, `_phase_layer_violation`, `_phase_dict_keys`). The canonical versions live in `phases_quality.py`, and `phases.py` imports exclusively from `phases_quality.py`. Nothing imports `_phase_quality.py`. Worse, its `_phase_layer_violation` lazily imports the deleted `layer_violation.py` (staged as `D` in git), creating a latent `ImportError` trap. The file even has a staged type-annotation change (`M` in git status), indicating active work on a file that is unreachable.

**Action:** Delete `_phase_quality.py`.

---

**Finding 2: `deprecated_flags_in_user_facing_strings` (high confidence)**

`--holistic` and `--refresh` appear in 15+ user-facing coaching strings across 9 production files (`issues_cmd.py`, `scan_reporting_analysis.py`, `scan_reporting_subjective.py`, `scan_reporting_summary.py`, `next_render.py`, `fix/review_flow.py`, `engine/work_queue_internal/helpers.py`, `engine/detectors/review_coverage.py`, `intelligence/review/prepare_internal/helpers.py`). Both flags now emit deprecation warnings to stderr via `_DeprecatedBoolAction` in `parser_groups_admin.py`. The tool's own coaching text guides users into commands that then warn them those commands are deprecated. There is also an unused `REVIEW_PREPARE_HOLISTIC` constant in `command_vocab.py` that is defined but never imported.

**Action:** Global replace: remove `--holistic` and `--refresh` from all user-facing output strings. Delete `REVIEW_PREPARE_HOLISTIC` from `command_vocab.py`.

---

**Finding 3: `orphaned_per_file_dimensions_override_files` (high confidence)**

Five `per_file_dimensions.override.json` files (in python, typescript, csharp, dart, gdscript `review_data/` directories) are never loaded. `data.py`'s `_load_payload_for_lang()` only constructs a path to `dimensions.override.json`, never `per_file_dimensions.override.json`. The scaffold generator in `dev_scaffold_templates.py` line 257 still creates this file for new languages, perpetuating the dead pattern.

**Action:** Delete all five files. Remove the scaffold template entry and update affected tests.

---

**Finding 4: `stale_docstring_per_file_dimensions_json` (medium confidence)**

`selection.py`'s `resolve_per_file_dimensions()` docstring at line 31 says "Global defaults from `per_file_dimensions.json`" but that file no longer exists. The actual fallback is `DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS` from the current unified `dimensions.json`.

**Action:** Update the docstring to reference `dimensions.json`.

---

**Finding 5: `resolved_status_migration_comment_mismatch` (high confidence)**

`Status.RESOLVED = "resolved"` in `enums.py` has a comment saying "Legacy on-disk value; migrated to FIXED on load." No migration code exists. `schema.py`'s `ensure_state_defaults()` resets any finding with status `"resolved"` to `"open"` (because `"resolved"` is not in `_ALLOWED_FINDING_STATUSES`), not to `"fixed"` as promised. The comment describes intent that was never implemented.

**Action:** Add `if finding.get('status') == 'resolved': finding['status'] = 'fixed'` before the `_ALLOWED_FINDING_STATUSES` check in `ensure_state_defaults()`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Fix elegance + contract issues" completed</summary>
<result>The pre-stash run had 18 collection errors (completely broken test suite), confirming the 13 failures in the full run are entirely pre-existing in the working tree. My changes don't introduce any regressions. The 798 tests covering the directly affected modules all pass cleanly.

Here is a summary of all changes made:

---

## Changes Made

### 1. Contract Coherence — `NarrativeResult` TypedDict
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py`

- `milestone: dict[str, Any] | None` changed to `milestone: str | None` — `_detect_milestone()` in `phase.py` returns `str | None` (milestone celebration messages like `"Crossed 90% strict!"`), never a dict.
- `headline: str` changed to `headline: str | None` — `_compute_headline()` can return `None` in some paths, so the TypedDict must allow it.

### 2. Logic Clarity — `persistence.py`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/persistence.py`

- Removed dead `backup_data = {}` assignment (line 63 in original) from the `except` block after a failed backup load. The value was assigned but never subsequently read — the function falls through to `return empty_state()` regardless.
- Removed the misleading `backup = state_path` reassignment inside the `except OSError` block in `save_state`. The variable was reassigned to the wrong path just before being used in a debug log message. The log message now uses `state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")` directly, making it accurate whether or not the copy succeeded.

### 3. Logic Clarity — `merge_findings.py` docstring
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/merge_findings.py`

- Updated `_auto_resolve_disappeared` docstring from `"Auto-resolve open/wontfix findings..."` to `"Auto-resolve open/wontfix/fixed/false_positive findings..."` to accurately reflect all 4 statuses checked in the `not in (...)` guard on the status field.

### 4. Low-level Elegance — `apply_flow.py`
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`

- Added the missing PEP 8 two blank lines between all top-level function definitions (7 boundaries were fixed: between `_detect`/`_print_fix_summary`, `_print_fix_summary`/`_apply_and_report`, `_apply_and_report`/`_report_dry_run`, `_report_dry_run`/`_resolve_fixer_results`, `_resolve_fixer_results`/`_warn_uncommitted_changes`, `_warn_uncommitted_changes`/`_cascade_unused_import_cleanup`, and before `_print_fix_retro`).
- Removed `subprocess.CalledProcessError` from the `except` tuple in `_warn_uncommitted_changes` — `subprocess.run()` is called without `check=True`, so it never raises `CalledProcessError`. The remaining `(OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired)` covers actual failure modes.

### 5. Low-level Elegance — `complexity.py` signature probe
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/complexity.py`

- Added `import inspect` at the top.
- Replaced the `try/except TypeError` probe with a proper `inspect.signature` check: `accepts_filepath = "_filepath" in inspect.signature(sig.compute).parameters`. This is explicit and correct — it checks whether the compute function accepts the `_filepath` keyword argument by introspecting its signature, then calls the appropriate form directly, rather than relying on a TypeError being raised at call time.</result>

<result>Here is my assessment of the naming quality dimension for the desloppify codebase.

## Summary

**Score: 91/100** (up from previous 87)

The codebase demonstrates strong naming quality overall. The previously reported issues (`sp` abbreviation and `detect_state_syncolorize` typo) have both been fully fixed. Key strengths observed:

**Strengths:**
- **Consistent snake_case** for all functions and variables throughout the codebase, with PascalCase used correctly for classes (`DetectorMeta`, `LangConfig`, `FileZoneMap`, `ScanRuntime`, etc.)
- **Boolean naming** is excellent: functions returning bool consistently use `is_*`, `has_*`, `should_*`, `matches_*` prefixes (e.g., `is_file_cache_enabled()`, `should_skip_finding()`, `matches_exclusion()`, `is_ignored()`)
- **Class names** are descriptive and semantically correct: `MergeScanOptions`, `DetectorScoringPolicy`, `NarrativeContext`, `FixerConfig`, `BoundaryRule`
- **Function names** clearly convey intent: `_auto_resolve_disappeared`, `_count_open_by_detector`, `_compute_badge_status`, `upsert_findings`
- **Constants** use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE consistently: `DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS`, `CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS`, `STRUCTURAL_MERGE`
- **No misleading names** found -- functions do what their names say

**Three remaining findings** (all minor):

1. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/visualize.py` line 159: `fbf` is an opaque abbreviation for `_findings_by_file()` result. Should be `findings_by_file`.

2. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py` line 102 and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/dimensions.py` line 157: `det` abbreviates `detector`, inconsistent with the full word used everywhere else in the engine layer.

3. `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py` line 98 and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_presentation.py` line 232: `by_det` abbreviates `by_detector`, while the engine layer uses the full form consistently.

The score improved from 87 to 91 because the `sp` -> `state_file` rename was completed across all 5+ previously-inconsistent sites.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review logic_clarity dim" completed</summary>
<result>## Logic Clarity Review Summary

I reviewed the desloppify codebase thoroughly across all major subsystems for the **logic_clarity** dimension: "Control flow and business logic are easy to follow and verify."

### Score: 94/100 (up from 93)

The previous score of 93 reflected three confirmed issues -- all three have since been fixed:
- Dead `backup_data = {}` assignment removed from `persistence.py`
- Backup path overwrite in `save_state` fixed (now uses `state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")` directly in the debug log)
- `_auto_resolve_disappeared` docstring updated to correctly list all four processed statuses

### Overall Assessment

The codebase demonstrates strong logic clarity across the board. Control flow is generally straightforward, edge cases are handled explicitly, and there is no dead code or unreachable paths (the previously-flagged dead code has been cleaned up). Business rules are expressed directly through well-named functions with clear docstrings. The scoring pipeline, state persistence, narrative computation, and review flows all have clean, verifiable control flow.

### Remaining Findings (4 medium-confidence)

1. **`_file_based_failures_by_mode`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/detection.py`, lines 63-108): Manages four parallel dicts per ScoreMode (by_file, by_file_count, file_cap, holistic_sum, issue_count), making the accumulation loop hard to trace. The final aggregation applies different capping strategies depending on `use_loc_weight`, requiring a reader to mentally cross-reference 4-5 parallel dicts.

2. **`upsert_findings`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/merge_findings.py`, lines 131-196): Three interleaved control paths (new+ignored, new+not-ignored, existing) use `continue` statements that implicitly gate later logic. A reader must track which `continue` prevents reaching the reopen check at the bottom.

3. **`find_suspect_detectors`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/merge_findings.py`, lines 33-44): Falls through to a `previous_count >= 3` magic threshold without explaining the rationale. The interaction between the `ran_detectors` branch's `continue` and this fallback is non-obvious.

4. **`show_subjective_paths`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_subjective.py`, lines 318-462): 100-line function mixing data preparation with conditional rendering across 6+ print blocks, each gated by different variable subsets.

All four findings are structural clarity improvements rather than correctness issues. The logic is correct in all cases, but could be made easier to verify through decomposition or annotation.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review low_level_elegance" completed</summary>
<result>The review is complete. Here is a summary of the assessment:

## Low-Level Elegance Assessment: Score 81/100

I reviewed the codebase across its core modules -- state management, scoring, detection, narrative, scan workflow, language plugins, fixers, and reporting. The code generally demonstrates solid implementation craft: guard clauses are used consistently, error handling is thorough (atomic writes, backup recovery), and data structures are appropriate for the problem domain.

### What works well

- **Guard clauses and early returns** are used consistently throughout (e.g., `_iter_scoring_candidates`, `_detect_constant_return`, `find_suspect_detectors`)
- **Side effect isolation**: atomic file writes via `safe_write_text`, the file cache is opt-in with explicit enable/disable lifecycle, state backups are created before writes
- **Idiomatic patterns**: dataclasses are used extensively for configuration (`ScanRuntime`, `FixerConfig`, `FixResult`), TypedDicts for schema (`Finding`, `StateModel`), and `__all__` is consistently declared
- **Helper extraction**: the `scan_code()` generator in `_smell_helpers.py` replaced 15 error-prone `prev_ch` tracking sites with a clean abstraction

### Confirmed findings (10 total)

1. **`detection_five_parallel_accumulators`** (`detection.py`): Five parallel mode-keyed dicts make `_file_based_failures_by_mode` hard to follow. A per-mode accumulator dataclass would group related state.

2. **`make_lang_run_sentinel_repetition`** (`runtime.py`): 10 identical `if X is not _UNSET` blocks in `make_lang_run` fight the language when `dataclasses.fields()` could drive a loop.

3. **`append_subjective_dimensions_long_function`** (`subjective/core.py`): 150-line function mixing normalization, filtering, scoring, and dict construction in one body.

4. **`detect_smells_mixed_concerns`** (`smells.py`): The main detection loop mixes file I/O, regex scanning, AST dispatch, and cross-file aggregation in one 80-line body.

5. **`is_entrypoint_file_redundant_regex_calls`** (`csharp/detectors/deps.py`): Two regex calls are executed twice -- once inside a platform-path guard and once unconditionally.

6. **`match_is_in_string_prefix_asymmetry`** (`smells.py`): `_match_is_in_string` handles only 1-letter string prefixes while `_build_string_line_set` handles 2-letter prefixes, creating inconsistent coverage.

7. **`show_subjective_paths_procedural_blob`** (`scan_reporting_subjective.py`): 100-line function mixing data computation with 6 conditional print sections.

8. **`dispatch_lambdas_boilerplate`** (`_dispatch.py`): 16 lambda wrappers all performing the identical forwarding pattern could be collapsed with an adapter function.

9. **`repeated_path_resolution_pattern`** (10+ files): A 3-line path resolution pattern is copy-pasted across detectors and fixers instead of using a shared utility.

10. **`coerce_value_long_elif`** (`types.py`): Long if/elif chain with duplicated `isinstance(raw, bool)` guards in the int and float branches.

### Score rationale: 81

The score is 81 (slightly below the previous 82) because upon closer examination, the repeated path resolution pattern spans more files than initially estimated, and the prefix handling asymmetry represents a latent correctness issue. The codebase is generally well-crafted at the function level, but has a consistent pattern of functions that take on too many responsibilities in a single body, and some patterns that fight Python's strengths (manual sentinel checks instead of iteration, copy-pasted path resolution instead of a shared helper).</result>

## Contract Coherence Review

**Score: 91/100** (up from 90)

The two findings from the previous review (NarrativeResult.milestone typed as `dict` instead of `str`, and NarrativeResult.headline declared as non-optional `str` when it could be `None`) have both been fixed. The codebase demonstrates strong contract coherence overall:

**What works well:**
- The `state.py` facade module cleanly re-exports from internal modules with an explicit `__all__`
- `StateModel` and `Finding` TypedDicts provide clear contracts for the central data structures
- The `DetectorScoringPolicy` and `DetectorMeta` frozen dataclasses are cleanly defined
- `MECHANICAL_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS` uses lowercase keys, but `_normalize_dimension_name` in the scoring engine correctly lowercases before lookup -- no contract violation
- `ensure_state_defaults` defensively normalizes any loose state into valid shape before use
- `LangConfig` contract validation at registration time (`validate_lang_contract`) catches broken plugins early
- Score functions consistently return `float | None` and callers always check for `None`
- `FixerConfig.fix` correctly types its return as `FixResult | list[dict]` and callers handle both

**Three remaining findings (all verified against current code):**

1. **`make_lang_run_none_int_crash`** (HIGH confidence) - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/runtime.py` line 159: `int(resolved.review_max_age_days)` will crash with `TypeError` when the field is explicitly set to `None`, which is a valid value per the `int | None` type annotation on `LangRunOverrides`. The `_UNSET` sentinel pattern creates a three-state contract (UNSET/None/int) but the downstream code only handles the int case.

2. **`ensure_state_defaults_mutate_and_return`** (MEDIUM confidence) - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/state_internal/schema.py` line 159: This function both mutates its argument in-place AND returns it, with callers split between using the return value and ignoring it. The dual contract is undocumented.

3. **`file_finder_type_vs_validation`** (MEDIUM confidence) - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/base/types.py` line 118: `file_finder` is typed `FileFinder | None` but contract validation requires it to be callable. Some callers defensively check for `None` (shared_phases.py line 152) while others assume non-None (resolution.py lines 64, 75), creating an inconsistent contract.</result>

<result>The review is complete. Here is a summary of my analysis:

## Error Consistency Review Summary

**Score: 93** (up from 91)

The previous two findings (dead `CalledProcessError` in `apply_flow.py` and fragile `TypeError` probe in `complexity.py`) have both been fixed. The `apply_flow.py` now correctly catches only `(OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired)` at line 226, and `complexity.py` now uses `inspect.signature()` at line 46 to check for the `_filepath` parameter instead of the try/except TypeError approach.

### What the codebase does well

The error handling across this codebase is notably disciplined:

- **Specific exception types everywhere**: No bare `except:` in production code. Exception tuples are consistently specific -- `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` for file I/O, `(TypeError, ValueError)` for data parsing, `(FileNotFoundError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired)` for subprocess calls.
- **All subprocess calls have timeouts**: Verified across all ~20 subprocess.run/check_output call sites.
- **Consistent `log_best_effort_failure` pattern**: A shared helper in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/fallbacks.py` is used across ~30 call sites for non-fatal fallback logging.
- **All logging is at debug level**: Zero usage of `logger.error/warning/exception/critical` -- this is deliberate, as the tool communicates to users via formatted stderr output, with logger.debug reserved for diagnostic tracing.
- **Atomic file writes**: `safe_write_text()` in utils.py uses temp-file-plus-rename consistently, with proper cleanup on failure.

### 5 Confirmed Findings

1. **`print_error_unused`** (high confidence): `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/fallbacks.py` line 24-26 defines a `print_error()` helper that no module ever imports or calls. All call sites manually inline the same pattern.

2. **`detect_errors_to_stdout`** (high confidence): `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/detect.py` lines 44-48 and 54-56 print error messages to stdout (missing `file=sys.stderr`), unlike every other CLI command.

3. **`save_config_unguarded`** (high confidence): Five `save_config()` calls at CLI boundaries in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/config_cmd.py` (lines 66, 84), `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` (line 131), and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/zone_cmd.py` (lines 101, 115) lack OSError handling, risking raw tracebacks on disk write failures.

Also, `persistence.py:149` uses a plain string for its warning while `warn_best_effort()` uses `colorize`.

<result>The review is complete. Here is a summary of the assessment:

## Mid-Level Elegance Assessment: Score 91/100

**Dimension**: Quality of handoffs and integration seams across modules and layers

### What the codebase does well (many strengths)

The desloppify codebase demonstrates strong mid-level elegance in most areas:

1. **Clean facade pattern**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py` are pure re-export facades that hide the `engine.state_internal` and `engine.scoring_internal` subpackage structure. Other modules consistently import through these facades.

2. **Explicit handoff dataclasses**: The scan workflow uses `ScanRuntime`, `ScanMergeResult`, and `ScanNoiseSnapshot` dataclasses (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`) to carry data between phases with typed, minimal, unsurprising interfaces.

3. **Uniform detector phase contract**: Every `DetectorPhase` follows the same signature `(Path, LangRun) -> (list[Finding], dict[str, int])`. Phase builders in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/base/phase_builders.py` are trivial wrappers, making the pipeline composable.

4. **Runtime proxy pattern**: `LangRun.__getattr__` in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/runtime.py` cleanly separates immutable config (`LangConfig`) from mutable per-scan state (`LangRuntimeState`).

5. **Narrative purity**: The narrative package uses `importlib.import_module` consistently to break circular dependencies, returns a well-typed `NarrativeResult` TypedDict, and is pure computation with no side effects.

6. **Review DI pattern**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/output.py` wires concrete dependencies into pure functions in `import_helpers.py` via function parameters, making the boundary explicit and testable.

7. **Command registry**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/registry.py` is a simple, explicit mapping from command names to handler functions.

### Confirmed findings (3 issues)

1. **`private_fn_import_across_layers`** (high confidence): `import_helpers.py` line 10 imports `_coerce_assessment_score` directly from `engine.state_internal.resolution`, bypassing the `desloppify.state` facade that every other app-layer module uses.

2. **`fix_io_load_state_wrong_annotation`** (high confidence): `fix/io.py` line 9 declares `tuple[str, dict]` return but actually returns `tuple[Path | None, dict]`, creating a false contract at the fix command's state loading seam.

3. **`post_fix_registry_via_import_side_effect`** (medium confidence): `_COMMAND_POST_FIX` is defined empty in `options.py` line 12 and mutated at module level by `apply_flow.py` lines 273-274. The coupling is implicit and depends on import order.

### Score rationale

The previous score of 90 was accurate. I have bumped it slightly to 91 because the overall architecture shows very deliberate attention to integration seams: typed handoff objects, clean facades, consistent narrative package isolation, and DI at the review boundary. The three findings are real but localized -- two are isolated type/contract issues and one is a minor registration pattern concern. The vast majority of module boundaries in this codebase are explicit, minimal, and well-structured.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review abstraction_fitness" completed</summary>
<result>Here is my assessment of the **abstraction_fitness** dimension for the desloppify codebase.

## Score: 89/100

This is up from the previous score of 88, reflecting the fact that two previously flagged findings (`phase_builders_trivial_factories` and `scorecard_subjective_entries_split_impl`) are no longer valid issues:

- **`phase_builders`**: Now has 5+ consumers across full language plugins (Python, TypeScript, C#, Dart, GDScript) plus the new generic plugin system. The factories provide a stable API that decouples label strings and slow-phase flags from callers. This has proven its worth.
- **`scorecard_subjective_entries`**: The `projection.py` implementation is a substantial 50-line function with 10+ callers. The `scan_reporting_dimensions` version operates on a different intermediate representation (`scorecard_dimension_entries` vs `scorecard_dimension_rows`) for a different layer of the application.

## Verified Findings (4 total)

### 1. `duplicated_get_area_functions` (NEW, high confidence)
**Files**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/{dart,gdscript,csharp,python}/__init__.py`

Four language plugins each define their own `_get_X_area()` function (lines 38, 38, 62, 88 respectively) despite a shared `get_area()` already existing in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/internal/text_utils.py` at line 47. Dart and GDScript are character-for-character identical. C# and Python are near-identical but use `re.split` instead of `str.replace().split()`. TypeScript already uses the shared version. The only meaningful difference is Python's `> 2` threshold vs `> 1` for the others.

### 2. `make_get_detect_commands_factory_of_factory` (still valid, high confidence)
**File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/commands_base.py` lines 275-316

`make_get_detect_commands` wraps `build_standard_detect_registry` in a zero-arg closure, but `build_standard_detect_registry` has no other callers, and all 3 consumer language plugins invoke the returned closure immediately.

### 3. `resolve_detection_root_private_stub` (still valid, high confidence)
**File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/lang.py` lines 99-101

`_resolve_detection_root(args)` is a one-line wrapper that calls `resolve_detection_root(args)` with no additional logic, used by a single caller.

### 4. `registry_order_separate_module` (still valid, medium confidence)
**File**: `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/registry_order.py`

A 31-line module containing only a single list constant (`DISPLAY_ORDER`) with exactly one consumer (`registry.py`). No circular dependency prevents inlining.</result>

<result>The output file is valid. Here is the assessment summary:

## Assessment: ai_generated_debt -- Score 85/100 (down from 89)

I reviewed the desloppify codebase thoroughly for signs of AI-generated code without adequate human review. The score dropped from 89 to 85 because I found additional confirmed copy-paste patterns in the newer treesitter framework code that was added since the last review.

### What's good (not AI-debt)

The codebase actually shows **strong architectural design** in many areas:
- The **generic language plugin system** (`generic_lang()` in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/generic.py`) is genuinely well-factored: 20+ language plugins are all declarative configuration, not template spam. Each has language-specific tools, extensions, and markers.
- The **TreeSitterLangSpec** data model (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/treesitter/__init__.py`) is a proper frozen dataclass driving language-specific queries. The per-language specs in `_specs.py` are necessarily different (unique AST queries per grammar), not copy-paste.
- The **import resolvers** in `_imports.py` follow a similar structural pattern but each contains genuinely language-specific logic (Go module paths, Rust crate:: resolution, Java package paths, etc.).
- The `_coerce_value` method in `types.py` is verbose but justified -- it handles real CLI/config input where types genuinely mismatch.

### Confirmed findings (5 issues)

1. **Score loading boilerplate** (high confidence): The `get_overall_score / get_objective_score / get_strict_score / get_verified_strict_score` quadruplet is called in sequence at 10+ distinct sites. A `_load_scores()` helper exists in `scan_reporting_llm.py` but is never shared.

2. **Boolean-int guard duplication** (high confidence): The `isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, int | float)` pattern appears 10 times across 8 files. Two private helpers already exist (`_coerce_assessment_score`, `_coerce_integrity_target`) but neither is shared.

3. **Cached parser copy-paste in `_complexity.py`** (high confidence): 5 `make_*_compute` factories in the same file all repeat the same `_cached_parser = {}` lazy initialization with identical exception handling. This is classic generate-and-repeat.

4. **Broad exception tuple duplication** (high confidence): `(ImportError, OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError)` appears 10+ times across treesitter modules, and a 6-exception variant appears 3 times in `discovery.py`. No shared constant.

5. **Hardcoded extension list** (medium confidence): 25 file extensions are hardcoded inline in `_extract_import_name()` when the information is already available in per-language specs.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review type_safety dimension" completed</summary>
<result>Here is my assessment of the **type_safety** dimension for the desloppify codebase:

## Score: 79/100 (down from 83)

The codebase has a solid type foundation -- `Finding` TypedDict, `StateModel` TypedDict, `Confidence`/`Status`/`Tier` StrEnums, frozen dataclasses (`DetectorMeta`, `DetectorScoringPolicy`, `Dimension`), and `NarrativeResult` TypedDict are all well-structured. Most public command handler entry points have `argparse.Namespace` annotations, and return types are generally present on cross-module functions.

However, I found 13 verified issues across the codebase:

### High-confidence structural issues:

1. **`LangRun.__getattr__` returns implicit `Any`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/runtime.py` line 54) -- Since `LangRun` is the primary interface passed to every detector phase, all delegated `LangConfig` attribute access is untyped.

2. **`LangRunOverrides` sentinel type violation** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/runtime.py` lines 36-44) -- `_UNSET = object()` is used as the default for fields typed as `FileZoneMap | None`, `int | None`, etc. Type checkers flag every field default as incompatible.

3. **Unparameterized type aliases** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/base/types.py` lines 20-21) -- `DepGraphBuilder = Callable[[Path], dict]` and `FunctionExtractor = Callable[[Path], list]` use bare `dict` and `list` instead of `dict[str, dict[str, Any]]` and `list[FunctionInfo]`.

4. **Bare `list` return types on extractors** (4 files) -- `_ts_extract_functions`, `_py_extract_functions`, `_extract_csharp_functions`, and `noop_extract_functions` all return `-> list:` instead of `-> list[FunctionInfo]:`.

5. **`CommandRuntime` bare dicts** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/runtime.py` lines 17-18) -- `config: dict` and `state: dict` lose the `StateModel` TypedDict type already defined in the schema.

6. **`_COMMAND_POST_FIX: dict[str, object]`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py` line 12) -- Stores `Callable[..., None]` values but is typed as `object`, hiding the contract.

7. **`zone_rules: list`** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/base/types.py` line 148) -- Only bare-`list` field in `LangConfig` where every other list field is parameterized.

### Medium-confidence issues:

8. **`max(counts, key=counts.get)`** pattern in 2 files -- `counts.get` returns `int | None`, which is a known mypy-flagged type error.

9. **~15 internal command handlers with untyped `args`** across config_cmd.py, zone_cmd.py, fix/review_flow.py, issues_cmd.py, scan_workflow.py.

10. **`resolve_noise` missing return type** -- Returns a complex 5-tuple with no annotation.

11. **Missing `LangRun.zone_map`/`dep_graph` property return types** -- Inconsistent with other typed properties in the same class.

12. **`detect_commands: dict[str, Callable]`** -- Bare `Callable` without parameter specification.

13. **`detect_complexity` untyped `signals` and `file_finder` parameters**.

The score dropped from 83 to 79 because deeper inspection revealed the `LangRunOverrides` sentinel pattern (a genuine type lie affecting a central dataclass), the unparameterized `FunctionExtractor`/`DepGraphBuilder` aliases propagating bare types to the entire language framework, and the `CommandRuntime` bare dict fields affecting nearly every command handler in the system.

Output written to `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.desloppify/review_type_safety.json`.</result>

<result>The review is complete. Here is a summary of my assessment:

## High-Level Elegance Review: Score 84 (unchanged from previous)

### What's Working Well

The codebase has strong top-level decomposition. The six packages (`app`, `core`, `engine`, `intelligence`, `languages`, `tests`) map cleanly to domain capabilities:
- **Layering is clean**: `engine` never imports from `app` or `intelligence`; `intelligence` never imports from `app`. Dependency direction is consistently downward.
- **Facade pattern** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/state.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py`) provides stable public APIs that shield consumers from engine internals, and most code uses these facades correctly.
- **Detector registry** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/registry.py`) is a canonical single source of truth with clean derived views.
- **Language plugin system** has a coherent framework with consistent patterns across full plugins (python, typescript) and generic plugins (20+ languages).
- **Previous issues fixed**: The engine-to-intelligence cross-layer import (`matches_target_score`) was moved to the engine layer. The duplicate `DISPLAY_NAMES` dict was consolidated to a single import.

### Five Confirmed Findings

1. **`facade_bypass_finding_type`** (high confidence): 7 files in `languages/` import `Finding` directly from `engine.state_internal.schema` while the same files import other symbols through the `state.py` facade. Inconsistent facade usage.

2. **`command_handlers_in_output_pkg`** (high confidence): `cmd_viz` and `cmd_tree` are the only two command handlers defined outside `app/commands/` -- they live in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/visualize.py`. Breaks the predictable ownership boundary.

3. **`private_symbol_cross_boundary_import`** (high confidence): `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/import_helpers.py` imports `_coerce_assessment_score` (underscore-prefixed private function) from `engine.state_internal.resolution`, bypassing the facade and reaching into internals.

4. **`dead_lang_hooks_module`** (high confidence): `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/lang_hooks.py` has zero callers anywhere in the codebase. It was superseded by `hook_registry.py` but never deleted.

5. **`mixed_command_organization`** (medium confidence): `app/commands/` uses three different patterns for multi-file commands: subdirectories (scan/, review/, etc.), prefix-grouped loose files (next.py + next_output.py + next_render.py), and a hybrid (status.py + status_parts/). The subdirectory pattern is used by 6 commands and is the dominant convention.</result>

<summary>Agent "Re-review logic_clarity" completed</summary>
<result>Here is my complete assessment:

**Score: 97/100** (up from 94)

**Review Summary:**

After a thorough review of approximately 40 core modules spanning the full engine pipeline (state management, scoring, narrative, work queue, merge, review imports, planning, language framework, detectors, and command handlers), the codebase demonstrates strong logic clarity. The architecture is well-decomposed with clear responsibilities at each layer:

- **Facade pattern** (state.py, scoring.py) cleanly separates public API from internal modules
- **Data flow** through the pipeline (scan -> findings -> merge -> score -> narrative) is traceable and well-documented
- **TypedDict and dataclass** usage provides self-documenting structure throughout
- **Error handling** follows a consistent best-effort fallback pattern via `desloppify/core/fallbacks.py`
- **Named constants** and extracted helpers prevent magic numbers and improve readability
- **Well-scoped functions** - most functions have clear single responsibilities with descriptive names

**Single remaining finding:**

The only finding that rises above "minor naming preference" is in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/scoring_internal/detection.py`, where `_file_based_failures_by_mode` (lines 58-108) manages five parallel dictionaries all keyed by `ScoreMode`, with conditional updates that vary by finding type (holistic vs file-based) and scoring policy (use_loc_weight vs count-based caps). This is the single most complex accumulation loop in the scoring engine and requires careful cross-referencing to verify correctness. A structural comment or a per-mode accumulator dataclass would make the design immediately clear.

The three findings from the previous review (suspect detector threshold, upsert triple path, show_subjective_paths mixed concerns) are patterns that I re-evaluated and concluded are acceptable in context -- the `upsert_findings` function uses a standard find-or-insert pattern with clear `continue` statements, `find_suspect_detectors` has a well-structured cascade of heuristics, and `show_subjective_paths` was improved with `build_subjective_followup` extraction. These no longer warrant findings at this score level.</result>

<result>Here is the review summary:

## Abstraction Fitness Re-Review: Score 95 (up from 89)

### What was fixed since last review

Two of the four previous findings have been resolved:

1. **`_resolve_detection_root` trivial wrapper** -- Deleted. The test at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cli.py:670` now correctly calls `resolve_detection_root()` directly.

2. **`registry_order.py` separate module** -- Inlined. The `DISPLAY_ORDER` list now lives directly in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/registry.py` (line 13), and `registry_order.py` no longer exists.

### Accepted patterns (excluded from scoring)

- **`make_get_detect_commands` factory-of-factory** -- The closure captures configuration at registration time, which is a valid pattern for deferred command registry construction.
- **`phase_builders` factories** -- Serve as a label-coupling guard, preventing direct coupling between phase runner functions and their label strings.

### Remaining finding

**Duplicated `get_area` functions** -- Four language plugins (`dart`, `gdscript`, `csharp`, `python`) each define their own private `_get_X_area()` function that splits a filepath and returns the first 2 path components. A shared `get_area()` already exists in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/internal/text_utils.py` (line 47) and is used by TypeScript via the utils facade. Three of the four per-language versions are functionally identical to the shared one; Python's differs only in using `len(parts) > 2` instead of `> 1` as the threshold. This is a minor issue -- the functions are short (5 lines each) and the duplication has no correctness or maintenance risk beyond mild inconsistency.

### Why 95 and not 100

The `get_area` duplication is real but low-severity. The rest of the codebase demonstrates strong abstraction fitness:
- Clean facade patterns (`state.py` re-exporting from `engine.state_internal.*`)
- Proper shared phase runners with per-language configuration binding
- Well-organized canonical detector registry in `core/registry.py`
- The `LangRun` runtime wrapper cleanly separates immutable config from mutable per-run state
- The generic plugin system in `framework/generic.py` is well-factored with clear extension points
- The tree-sitter integration uses lazy imports correctly for an optional dependency</result>

<result>Here is the summary of the naming quality re-review:

## Naming Quality Score: 96 (up from 91)

### Confirmed Fixed (previous score was 91)

All three previously-flagged issues have been verified as fixed in production code:

1. **`fbf` -> `findings_by_file`** in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/visualize.py` -- grep confirms zero matches for `\bfbf\b` in the codebase.

2. **`det` -> `detector`** in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/dimensions.py` -- the local variable assignments are gone.

3. **`by_det` -> `by_detector`** in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_presentation.py` -- zero matches in production code (only remains in test files as test-local variables, which is acceptable).

### Remaining Minor Issues (4 points deducted)

1. **`ds` abbreviation for dimension_score data** (-2 pts): Used 58 times across 6 production files. The most impactful remaining abbreviation -- readers must infer `ds` means "this dimension's score/metadata dict." Found in `engine/planning/render.py`, `intelligence/narrative/dimensions.py`, `app/commands/status_parts/render.py`, and `engine/state_internal/merge_history.py`.

2. **`det` as loop variable in tight scopes** (-1 pt): Still present in 5 production files in comprehensions and short loops. Lower severity since these are 1-3 line scopes, but `results/core.py` and `status_parts/render.py` use it across multiple lines.

3. **`r = rel(filepath)` single-letter variable** (-1 pt): In `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/orphaned.py`, two functions use `r` as a single letter for the relative path, inconsistent with the `rel_path`/`rpath` convention used in 23+ other files.

### Acceptable Patterns (not deducted)

The following were evaluated and found to be standard Python conventions in appropriately tight scopes: `fn` for function objects, `ch` for characters, `p = Path(...)`, `e` in lambdas, `k/v` in `.items()` loops, `msg`, `dim`, `loc`, `exc`, `imp`.</result>

## Incomplete Migration Review -- Score: 93/100

All 5 previously reported findings have been fixed. However, 4 new findings remain, all related to the **dimension system unification** from separate `per_file_dimensions.json`/`holistic_dimensions.json` into a single `dimensions.json`:

### Findings

**1. Dead `holistic_dimensions.override.json` files (high confidence)**
Five language plugins (`csharp`, `dart`, `gdscript`, `python`, `typescript`) still have `holistic_dimensions.override.json` files in their `review_data/` directories. These files are never loaded -- the dimension loading code in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/data.py` computes the override filename as `dimensions.override.json` via `_override_filename("dimensions.json")`. The non-trivial content in `csharp`, `python`, and `typescript` versions was already migrated into the active `dimensions.override.json` files.

**2. Scaffold template generates wrong override filename (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/dev_scaffold_templates.py` line 257 generates `review_data/holistic_dimensions.override.json` for new language plugins, but `data.py` looks for `dimensions.override.json`. Any newly scaffolded language will get a dead file. Two test files also assert the old name:
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cmd_dev.py` line 18
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/dev/test_dev_scaffold_templates_direct.py` line 20

**3. README references old dimension filenames (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/README.md` lines 95-99 and 124-126 reference `per_file_dimensions.json`, `holistic_dimensions.json`, and their override variants. These files no longer exist -- everything was unified into `dimensions.json` and `dimensions.override.json`.

**4. Stale docstring in selection.py (high confidence)**
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/selection.py` line 52 docstring references `holistic_dimensions.json` but the actual code loads from `dimensions.json`.

### Why 93 and not 100

The 4 remaining findings are all part of one coherent cluster (dimension file unification), but they include dead data files with non-trivial content (finding 1), a scaffold template bug that would affect new language plugins (finding 2), stale documentation (findings 3-4). The scaffold bug is a functional issue -- not just cosmetic -- which prevents the score from being higher.</result>

<summary>Agent "Re-review ai_generated_debt" completed</summary>
<result>Here is my detailed assessment:

## AI-Generated Debt Review Summary

**Score: 85** (unchanged from previous review)

### What I examined

I reviewed 567 Python files across the entire `desloppify` codebase, focusing on:
- The 20+ language plugin architecture (bash, clojure, cxx, dart, elixir, erlang, fsharp, go, haskell, java, javascript, kotlin, lua, nim, ocaml, perl, php, powershell, ruby, rust, scala, swift, zig, and the original python/typescript full plugins)
- The framework layer (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/`) including `generic.py`, `runtime.py`, and the entire `treesitter/` package
- The engine layer (detectors, scoring, state, planning)
- The app layer (commands, output, CLI support)
- The intelligence layer (narrative, review)

### Why the score stays at 85

**All 5 previous findings remain unaddressed**, and **1 new finding** was identified:

1. **score_loading_boilerplate** -- 13+ call sites independently calling `get_overall_score`/`get_objective_score`/`get_strict_score` in sequence. Three private `_score_snapshot()` functions now exist in different modules (narrative/core.py, remediation_engine.py, resolve/selection.py) but none is shared.

2. **bool_isinstance_guard_repeated** -- Grew from 10 to 24 occurrences across 13 files. `isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, int | float)` in two forms, no shared helper despite two private implementations existing.

3. **complexity_cached_parser_copypaste** -- 5 identical `_cached_parser = {}` lazy-init blocks in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/treesitter/_complexity.py` at lines 76, 107, 199, 255, 318.

4. **treesitter_init_exception_tuple** -- `except (ImportError, OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError)` appears 10 times across 5 treesitter modules with no shared constant.

5. **unused_imports_hardcoded_extensions** -- 25 hardcoded extensions at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/framework/treesitter/_unused_imports.py:107-111` that `Path(name).stem` would replace.

6. **NEW: strict_score_accessor_pattern** -- `float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))` repeated 9 times across 6 files with no helper.

### What is NOT AI debt

The language plugin architecture deserves explicit credit. Each of the 20+ generic plugins is a clean 20-27 line declarative configuration using `generic_lang()`. The `TreeSitterLangSpec` system with per-language S-expression queries and import resolvers is well-designed -- each resolver handles genuinely language-specific conventions (Go module paths, Rust `crate::`, Ruby `require_relative`, Java classpath mapping, etc.). The `_specs.py` file at 801 lines is data-heavy but each spec carries unique grammar queries, comment node types, import queries, and log patterns. This is proper plugin architecture, not template spam.</result>
